## Concept
One or two sentences in your own words. What does the player do, and why is it satisfying?

## Player
Describe what the player controls — the avatar, ship, cursor, paddle, whatever it is. Visually, what is it (one sentence)? List its verbs (move, jump, shoot, place, type). Keep it abstract — no coordinates, no speeds.

## Core loop
What happens every 1–3 seconds of normal play. Be specific about the moment-to-moment experience: what's the player reading, what are they choosing between, what makes them lean forward.

## Hazards / Obstacles
What stands in the player's way. Describe each kind by behavior and threat — "fast horizontal sweepers that telegraph their path", "stationary blocks that explode when shot". Concepts and behaviors, not numbers.

## Score
What discrete events increase the score. Examples: "survived a wave", "collected a pickup", "killed an enemy at long range". Avoid score = elapsed time as the only mechanic.

## Win and lose
When does the player win? When do they lose? What happens on screen the moment it ends.

## Feel
The vibe. Pace (frantic / steady / meditative). Aesthetic direction (neon / pastel / monochrome / retro CRT). Sound character (chiptune / arcade beeps / silent). One short paragraph.

## Why it's fun
One sentence. The single moment that would make a player smile, curse, or shout. What is this game's hook?

TASKS.md

show
1. Scaffold HTML and meta.json — create index.html with required header, canvas, restart button, empty script block, and matching meta.json with title, description, and controls.
2. Define initial game state objects — declare plain objects for player, hazards, score, phase, and overall state with initial values at top of script.
3. Implement animation loop — add startLoop() with cancelAnimationFrame handling, requestAnimationFrame loop that computes dt, wraps body in try/catch, and calls update(dt) and render().
4. Set up input handling — create a Set keys, add keydown/keyup listeners, map R key and restart button to a shared reset() function.
5. Add player movement — implement dt‑based physics for horizontal movement based on Arrow keys (or A/D), with acceleration, friction, speed cap, and canvas bounds.
6. Create hazard system — define hazard data structure, spawn rules respecting SAFE_RADIUS around player, and dt‑scaled motion.
7. Implement collision detection — detect player vs hazard collisions, transition state to "gameover", and stop the active gameplay loop.
8. Add scoring logic — increase score on discrete events (e.g., each hazard passed), and draw current score on canvas top‑left.
9. Render frame — clear canvas, draw background, draw hazards, draw player (high‑contrast), and draw HUD elements.
10. Game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions on canvas.
11. Expose observable contract — populate window.__game each frame with state ("menu" | "playing" | "gameover"), score, player position/visibility, and a one‑sentence objective.
12. Add audio effects — lazy‑initialize AudioContext with a beep() helper, and play sounds on hit, score, and death events.

    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error('loop error', err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    }

    startLoop();

Let's craft design:

## Concept
One line or two sentences: The player controls a dot that automatically moves forward along a sinusoidal path; tapping flips the wave's phase, letting the dot swing between peaks and troughs to slip through gaps in a scrolling tunnel.

## Player
Avatar: a small glowing dot; verbs: auto‑move forward, flip wave (phase) via tap/click; optionally pause? Actually only flip.

## Core loop
Every 1–3 seconds the tunnel scrolls towards the dot; the dot follows a sine wave; the player watches the upcoming gap and decides whether to tap to invert the wave; a successful alignment lets the dot pass the obstacle; after each obstacle the pattern repeats, increasing difficulty.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- Tunnel walls with one or more vertical gaps; gaps are static and positioned at varying heights.
- Occasionally narrow or offset gaps that require precise timing.
- Spike edges or lasers that appear at the wall boundaries for extra risk (optional).
No moving obstacles, but the speed is constant; maybe occasional moving block that slides horizontally.

## Score
- Each gap successfully passed increments score.
- Bonus points for consecutive flawless passes (combo).
- Optional: extra points for flipping at exact moment when gap aligns (perfect timing).

## Win and lose
- Lose when the dot collides with a wall or spikes.
- Win when the player reaches a target score or survives a set number of obstacles (e.g., 30 gaps); upon win a bright burst and “You Win!” overlay appears.

## Feel
A fast‑paced, reflex‑driven arcade vibe with neon‑glow colors against a dark tunnel; the music is a pulsing synth beat with succinct arcade beeps for taps and collisions. The visual rhythm matches the sine wave pattern, giving a sense of flow.

## Why it's fun
A single tap that instantly flips the wave lets the player feel a satisfying “just‑in‑time” moment when the dot lines up perfectly with a gap.

Make sure to keep within requested sections.

    const HAZARD_GAP = 80;

We'll replace this with full update logic.

Implementation details:

```
    function update(dt) {
      if (gameState !== 'playing') return;

      // Advance travel distance
      travel += WAVE_SPEED * dt;

      // Update player position (follow wave)
      player.y = canvas.height / 2 + WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin((travel * 2 * Math.PI / WAVELENGTH) + wavePhase);

      // Move hazards
      for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        const h = hazards[i];
        h.x -= WAVE_SPEED * dt;
        // Score when hazard passed player
        if (!h.passed && h.x + HAZARD_WIDTH < PLAYER_X) {
          h.passed = true;
          score++;
        }
        // Collision detection when hazard overlaps player
        if (h.x <= PLAYER_X && h.x + HAZARD_WIDTH >= PLAYER_X) {
          const withinGap = player.y >= h.gapY - HAZARD_GAP / 2 && player.y <= h.gapY + HAZARD_GAP / 2;
          if (!withinGap) {
            gameState = 'gameover';
            // optional: beep for death
            beep(110, 0.25);
          }
        }
        // Remove off-screen hazards
        if (h.x + HAZARD_WIDTH < 0) {
          hazards.splice(i, 1);
        }
      }

      // Spawn new hazards
      if (hazards.length === 0 || hazards[hazards.length - 1].x < canvas.width - HAZARD_SPACING) {
        const spawnX = canvas.width + HAZARD_WIDTH;
        const worldX = travel + (spawnX - PLAYER_X);
        const gapY = canvas.height / 2 + WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin((worldX * 2 * Math.PI / WAVELENGTH) + wavePhase);
        hazards.push({ x: spawnX, gapY, passed: false });
      }

      // Publish observable state
      publishState();
    }
```

But we need to define beep function (audio). The beep function may not be defined yet. We'll add beep later.

We'll replace with rendering code:

```
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Draw hazards (top and bottom rectangles)
      ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
      for (const h of hazards) {
        // Top part
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, 0, HAZARD_WIDTH, h.gapY - HAZARD_GAP / 2);
        // Bottom part
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.gapY + HAZARD_GAP / 2, HAZARD_WIDTH, canvas.height - (h.gapY + HAZARD_GAP / 2));
      }

      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(PLAYER_X, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();

      // Draw HUD (score)
      ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
      ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
      ctx.textAlign = 'left';
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);

      // Game over overlay
      if (gameState === 'gameover') {
        ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(10,10,15,0.7)';
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.textAlign = 'center';
        ctx.font = '44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
        ctx.fillText('Game Over', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.font = '20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
        ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillStyle = '#a5a5b8';
        ctx.fillText('Press R or click Restart', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
      }
    }
```

Thus new INPUT block:

```
    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    function togglePhase() {
      wavePhase = (wavePhase === 0) ? Math.PI : 0;
    }

    document.getElementById('restart').addEventListener('click', () => {
      reset();
    });

    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        reset();
      } else if (ev.key === ' ' || ev.key === 'Spacebar' || ev.code === 'Space') {
        togglePhase();
        // optional beep for flip
        beep(440, 0.1);
      } else {
        keys.add(ev.key);
      }
    });
    document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
      keys.delete(ev.key);
    });

    // Click or tap to flip wave phase
    canvas.addEventListener('click', () => {
      togglePhase();
      beep(440, 0.1);
    });
```

with (including new line for beep and the observable contract line unchanged):

```
    // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
    function beep(freq, durSec) {
      try {
        if (!window.audioCtx) window.audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
        const osc = window.audioCtx.createOscillator();
        const gain = window.audioCtx.createGain();
        osc.type = 'square';
        osc.frequency.value = freq;
        gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, window.audioCtx.currentTime);
        gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
        osc.connect(gain);
        gain.connect(window.audioCtx.destination);
        osc.start();
        osc.stop(window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      } catch (e) {
        // audio may be disabled
      }
    }

    // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================
```

function update(dt) {
      // Move player forward.
      player.x += WAVE_SPEED * dt;
      // Loop back to start if off screen.
      if (player.x > canvas.width + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
        player.x = -PLAYER_RADIUS;
      }
      // Update vertical position based on sine wave.
      player.y = canvas.height / 2 + wavePhase * WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin((player.x / WAVELENGTH) * Math.PI * 2);
      // Update score based on distance traveled.
      score = Math.floor(player.x / 10);
    }

function render() {
      // Clear canvas.
      ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

```
    function update(dt) {
      if (phase !== 'playing') return;
      // auto-advance
      player.x += WAVE_SPEED * dt;
      // vertical sinusoidal path
      const waveOffset = wavePhase * WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin((player.x / WAVELENGTH) * Math.PI * 2);
      player.y = canvas.height / 2 + waveOffset;

      // horizontal player control
      const dir = (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D') ? 1 : 0)
                - (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A') ? 1 : 0);
      const ACCEL = 600; // px/s^2
      const MAX_SPEED = 400; // cap
      const FRICTION = 800; // deceleration when no input
      // track horizontal velocity in a variable; need to define it.
```

Now update: apply acceleration if dir != 0, else apply friction to reduce vx toward 0. We'll then clamp vx to +/-MAX_SPEED.

```
      if (dir !== 0) {
        playerVX += dir * ACCEL * dt;
      } else {
        // apply friction
        if (playerVX > 0) {
          playerVX = Math.max(0, playerVX - FRICTION * dt);
        } else if (playerVX < 0) {
          playerVX = Math.min(0, playerVX + FRICTION * dt);
        }
      }
      // clamp speed
      playerVX = Math.max(-MAX_SPEED, Math.min(MAX_SPEED, playerVX));
      player.x += playerVX * dt;
```

Implement:

```
    function render() {
      // clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // draw wave path
      ctx.strokeStyle = '#7df9ff';
      ctx.lineWidth = 2;
      ctx.beginPath();
      for (let x = 0; x <= canvas.width; x++) {
        const y = canvas.height / 2 + wavePhase * WAVE_AMPLITUDE * Math.sin((x / WAVELENGTH) * Math.PI * 2);
        if (x === 0) ctx.moveTo(x, y);
        else ctx.lineTo(x, y);
      }
      ctx.stroke();
      // draw player dot
      ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
      // draw score
      ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
      ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
      ctx.textAlign = 'left';
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
      // publish state
      publishState();
    }
```

- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.

prompt (13,888 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

  // Reinitialize game state
  player = { x: 400, y: 300 };
  hazards = [];

}

- Add constants for movement: ACCEL = e.g., 800 pixels/sec^2, FRICTION = 600, MAX_SPEED = 500 (maybe). Use dt based.

Pseudo:

```
const PLAYER_ACCEL = 800; // px/sec^2
const PLAYER_FRICTION = 600; // px/sec^2
const PLAYER_MAX_SPEED = 500; // px/sec

function update(dt) {
  // horizontal movement
  let dir = 0;
  if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) dir += 1;
  if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) dir -= 1;
  // apply acceleration
  if (dir !== 0) {
    player.vx += dir * PLAYER_ACCEL * dt;
  } else {
    // apply friction to slow down
    if (player.vx > 0) {
      player.vx = Math.max(0, player.vx - PLAYER_FRICTION * dt);
    } else if (player.vx < 0) {
      player.vx = Math.min(0, player.vx + PLAYER_FRICTION * dt);
    }
  }
  // clamp speed
  if (player.vx > PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
  if (player.vx < -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
  // update position
  player.x += player.vx * dt;
  // bounds
  const halfWidth = 10; // scenario player's width? we can define a radius. Use clamp.
  player.x = Math.max(0, Math.min(canvas.width, player.x));
}
```

But we need to decide radius. Since not defined, just clamp within full canvas.

```
    // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================
    function publishState() {
      // Determine visibility: player within canvas bounds
      const visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= canvas.width && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= canvas.height;
      // Map internal phase to observable state
      const stateMap = {
        playing: 'playing',
        gameover: 'gameover',
        menu: 'menu',
      };
      const obsState = stateMap[phase] || 'menu';
      window.__game = {
        state: obsState,
        score,
        player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible },
        objective: 'Ride the dot through the sinusoidal tunnel, tapping to flip the wave phase and avoid obstacles.'
      };
    }
    // Initial publish to set __game
    publishState();
```

Title: Wave Ride
Concept: The dot auto‑advances while riding a sinusoidal vertical path; each tap flips the wave’s phase (upward vs downward) so you line up the peaks and troughs with gaps in the tunnel.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. ```

2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:wave-ride:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically.
